## Interfacial self\-healing polymer electrolytes for long\-cycle solid\-state lithium\-sulfur batteries

Paper: 10\.1038/s41467\-023\-43467\-w

#### Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

Poly\(ethylene oxide\) \(PEO, Mw = 6 × 10⁵ g mol⁻¹\) and lithium bis\(fluorosulfonyl\)imide \(LiFSI\) were dissolved in acetonitrile with a mass ratio of 2:1 \(PEO: LiFSI\) to form a uniform PEO/LiFSI solution\.

Specimen: PEO/LiFSI

Original evidence — main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src\-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

```text
Poly(ethylene oxide) (PEO, Mw = 6 × 10⁵ g mol⁻¹) and lithium bis(fluorosulfonyl)imide (LiFSI) were dissolved in acetonitrile with a mass ratio of 2:1 (PEO: LiFSI) to form a uniform PEO/LiFSI solution.
```

Quote matched to converted source text
Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts \(jats\_script\_notation\_v1\)\.
Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes\.

Specimen: PEO/LiFSI

Original evidence — main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src\-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

```text
These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.
```

#### Properties

Needs review

ionic conductivity: 1\.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹

Specimen: PEO/LiFSI

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```


#### Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

PTMG\-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI\.


Original evidence — main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG\-HDI, paragraph 25 · chunk src\-0e0464a320ed4ac7c4a6

```text
PTMG-HDI was synthesized via condensation of PTMG with HDI.
```

Quote matched to converted source text
Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts \(jats\_script\_notation\_v1\)\.
Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

Briefly, 2\.5 g \(1\.25 mmol\) of PTMG and 250 μL \(1\.5 mmol\) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂\. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst\. The final molar ratio of the monomers \(PTMG:HDI\) was 1:1\.2\. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂\-filled glove box\. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h\. Finally, a transparent PTMG\-HDI solution was obtained\.

Specimen: PTMG\-HDI solution

Original evidence — main · Methods / Preparation of PTMG\-HDI, paragraph 25 · chunk src\-0e0464a320ed4ac7c4a6

```text
Briefly, 2.5 g (1.25 mmol) of PTMG and 250 μL (1.5 mmol) of HDI were dissolved in 5 mL of CH₂Cl₂. 5 μL of DBTDL was added to the solution as a reaction catalyst. The final molar ratio of the monomers (PTMG:HDI) was 1:1.2. The mixture was stirred at 40 °C for 3 h in a N₂-filled glove box. Then, 5 mL of DMAc was added to the reaction mixture every 2 h 3 times repeated 3 times, and stirred for 12 h. Finally, a transparent PTMG-HDI solution was obtained.
```

Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

The PTMG\-HDI and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide \(DMAc\) at a mass ratio of 2:1 \(polymer: LiFSI\) and stirred for 3 h\. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes\.

Specimen: PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI

Original evidence — main · Methods / Preparation of the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG\-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes, paragraph 27 · chunk src\-6524f9582cedc0d6c138

```text
The PTMG-HDI and PTMG-HDI-BHDS polymer solutions were mixed with LiFSI in dimethylacetamide (DMAc) at a mass ratio of 2:1 (polymer: LiFSI) and stirred for 3 h. These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.
```

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```

Original evidence — main · Results / Material fabrication and characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 8 · chunk src\-8bc608870b143b549de5

```text
The real-time polymerization process of PTMG-HDI-BHDS was monitored, and the average molecular weight (M_{n}) and viscosity (η) were measured with gel permeation chromatography (GPC) (Fig. 2b). The viscosity increased synchronously with the molecular weight during a reaction time of 8 h at 40 °C, and M_{n} and η ultimately reached 7.3 × 10⁵ g mol⁻¹ and 1.3 × 10⁴ cps, respectively, which were significantly higher than those of PTMG-HDI.
```

Sample-link evidence — main · Results / Material fabrication and characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 8 · chunk src\-8bc608870b143b549de5

```text
The real-time polymerization process of PTMG-HDI-BHDS was monitored, and the average molecular weight (M_{n}) and viscosity (η) were measured with gel permeation chromatography (GPC) (Fig. 2b). The viscosity increased synchronously with the molecular weight during a reaction time of 8 h at 40 °C, and M_{n} and η ultimately reached 7.3 × 10⁵ g mol⁻¹ and 1.3 × 10⁴ cps, respectively, which were significantly higher than those of PTMG-HDI.
```

Source SHA-256: ffc5376b77b6b81e29c52134d1f96d0185752dd35d35cb48172b661b13c8e1a8

ionic conductivity: 2\.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹

Specimen: PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```

```text
The linear sweep voltammograms (LSV) for the asymmetrical cells indicated that PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI displayed a wider electrochemical voltage beyond 5.0 V than PEO (3.8 V) and PTMG-HDI (4.1 V), which is valuable for high-voltage (e.g., LiNi_{0.8}Co_{0.1}Mn_{0.1}O₂) battery systems, the corresponding PEO and PTMG-HDI showed significantly poor compatibility (Fig. 3e and Supplementary Fig. 17).
```

#### Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

The P\(MMA\-BA\-\(F\-MA\)\) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization\.


Original evidence — main · 2\. Materials and Methods / 2\.3\. Preparation of the Self\-Healing Polymer Precursor, paragraph 9 · chunk src\-189865c0a792b53247cf

```text
The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was prepared by emulsion polymerization.
```

Quote matched to converted source text
Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts \(jats\_script\_notation\_v1\)\.
Source SHA-256: cfd69ea12c067601a053396289614766ecd9d617fe86497483e61c116407fadc

The mixture \(MMA:BMA:F\-MA = 1:2:1 \(in molar ratio\)\) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution\. After adding ammonium persulfate \(as an initiator\) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂\(SO₄\)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water \(55 °C\) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier\. The P\(MMA\-BA\-\(F\-MA\)\) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation\.


Original evidence — main · 2\. Materials and Methods / 2\.3\. Preparation of the Self\-Healing Polymer Precursor, paragraph 9 · chunk src\-189865c0a792b53247cf

```text
The mixture (MMA:BMA:F-MA = 1:2:1 (in molar ratio)) was added to the above solution under vigorous stirring for 1 h to form an emulsified solution. After adding ammonium persulfate (as an initiator) and stirring continuously for 6 h, the resulting emulsion was poured into 3 wt% Al₂(SO₄)₃ solution to yield a precipitate that was isolated by filtration and washed with hot deionized water (55 °C) in order to remove any impurities such as residual monomers and emulsifier. The P(MMA-BA-(F-MA)) copolymer was finally obtained by drying the purified precipitate in a vacuum oven at 60 °C for 12 h and kept in a desiccator for membrane preparation.
```

```text
Initially, a homogeneous solution of copolymer and lithium salt was prepared by mixing the components at 50 °C for 1 h. ```
```

```text
To synthesize a bis-isocyanate-terminated preoligomer, α, ω-dihydroxyethylpropoxyl-PDMS (M_{w} = 4,600 g mol⁻¹, as a soft segment diol) was reacted with isophorone diisocyanate (IPDI) monomer (M_{(PDMS)}/M_{(IPDI)} = 1/2) in N, N^{′}-dimethylacetamide (DMAc) using dibutyltin dilaurate (DBTDL) as a catalyst.
```

Additional extracted facts need review; see the audit.
#### Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

A series of polymers were synthesized through a two\-step polycondensation reaction\. To synthesize a bis\-isocyanate\-terminated preoligomer, α, ω\-dihydroxyethylpropoxyl\-PDMS \(M\_\{w\} = 4,600 g mol⁻¹, as a soft segment diol\) was reacted with isophorone diisocyanate \(IPDI\) monomer \(M\_\{\(PDMS\)\}/M\_\{\(IPDI\)\} = 1/2\) in N, N^\{′\}\-dimethylacetamide \(DMAc\) using dibutyltin dilaurate \(DBTDL\) as a catalyst\. Then, 4,4^\{′\}\-dithiodianiline \(SS\) \(an aromatic disulfide\) and/or 4,4^\{′\}\-bis\(hydroxymethyl\)\-2,2^\{′\}\-bipyridine \(BNB\) were added to the solution as a chain extender to complete the PDMS–SS–IP–BNB synthesis\.


Original evidence — main · Results, paragraph 8 · chunk src\-08d3510e5447dda4de4b

```text
A series of polymers were synthesized through a two-step polycondensation reaction. To synthesize a bis-isocyanate-terminated preoligomer, α, ω-dihydroxyethylpropoxyl-PDMS (M_{w} = 4,600 g mol⁻¹, as a soft segment diol) was reacted with isophorone diisocyanate (IPDI) monomer (M_{(PDMS)}/M_{(IPDI)} = 1/2) in N, N^{′}-dimethylacetamide (DMAc) using dibutyltin dilaurate (DBTDL) as a catalyst. Then, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (SS) (an aromatic disulfide) and/or 4,4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (BNB) were added to the solution as a chain extender to complete the PDMS–SS–IP–BNB synthesis.
```

#### Composition

Reported in the supplied text

HO–PDMS–OH \(23\.0 g, 5\.0 mmol\); IPDI \(2\.44 g, 11 mmol\); DBTDL \(0\.08 g\); 4,4^\{′\}\-dithiodianiline \(0\.6 g, 2\.5 mmol\); 4, 4^\{′\}\-bis\(hydroxymethyl\)\-2,2^\{′\}\-bipyridine \(0\.465 g, 2\.5 mmol\); DMAc \(20 mL\); dry DMAc \(6 mL\); methanol \(3 mL\)

Basis: component\_amounts

Original evidence — main · Methods / Polymer synthesis, paragraph 29 · chunk src\-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83

```text
HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups.
```

#### Synthesis text

Reported in the supplied text

A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below\. HO–PDMS–OH \(23\.0 g, 5\.0 mmol\) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum \(&lt;133 Pa\) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C\. IPDI \(2\.44 g, 11 mmol\) and DBTDL \(0\.08 g\) were dissolved in DMAc \(20 mL\), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere\. After the synthesis of the pre\-polymer, 4,4^\{′\}\-dithiodianiline \(0\.6 g, 2\.5 mmol\) and 4, 4^\{′\}\-bis\(hydroxymethyl\)\-2,2^\{′\}\-bipyridine \(0\.465 g, 2\.5 mmol\) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre\-polymer solution\. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol \(3 mL\), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups\. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene \(PTFE\) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete\. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample \(16 g, yield 60%\) as a yellow brown elastomer\.


Original evidence — main · Methods / Polymer synthesis, paragraph 29 · chunk src\-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83

```text
A typical polymerization procedure for P3 is described below. HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups. Then, the solution was poured into a Poly tetra fluoroethylene (PTFE) plate that was subsequently put in a vacuum oven at 90 °C for 12 h to allow the reaction to complete. By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer.
```

Quote matched to converted source text
Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts \(jats\_script\_notation\_v1\)\.
Source SHA-256: b6e614f9b777ddcb51109ddfa334540b3cf9a0acd27d62cfa80d29caea3a6bd4

P3 \(1\.00 g\) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂\. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish \(diameter 3\.5 cm\) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight\. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h\. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h\. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing\.

Specimen: P3 film

Original evidence — main · Methods / Film preparation for self\-healing and mechanical property testing, paragraph 30 · chunk src\-3c26b5356cd78c6d5409

```text
P3 (1.00 g) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish (diameter 3.5 cm) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing.
```

The round polymer tube was prepared with 36 mm long, 1\.5 mm thick, and 2 mm inside diameter\.

The round polymer tube was prepared with 36 mm long, 1.5 mm thick, and 2 mm inside diameter.

#### Linked formulations and specimens

purified P3 sample

Original evidence — main · Methods / Polymer synthesis, paragraph 29 · chunk src\-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83

```text
By dissolving the crude polymer in 40 mL of CH₂Cl₂ and then precipitating it into petroleum ether, we successfully obtained the purified P3 sample (16 g, yield 60%) as a yellow brown elastomer. The purification process was repeated three times. GPC using THF as the eluent measured the average molecular weight as M_{n} = 63 KDa and M_{w} = 112 KDa, and M_{w}/M_{n} = 1.77.
```

Quote matched to converted source text
Source XML converted with preserved superscripts/subscripts \(jats\_script\_notation\_v1\)\.
Source SHA-256: b6e614f9b777ddcb51109ddfa334540b3cf9a0acd27d62cfa80d29caea3a6bd4

P3 film

Original evidence — main · Methods / Film preparation for self\-healing and mechanical property testing, paragraph 30 · chunk src\-3c26b5356cd78c6d5409

```text
P3 (1.00 g) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish (diameter 3.5 cm) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing.
```
